Nairobi, Munich to host 2029, 2031 World Athletics Championships
China
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
20:37:32 CST
21:37:32 KST

BUDAPEST, Sept. 15 (Xinhua) -- Nairobi and Munich will host the 2029 and 2031 World Athletics Championships respectively, with the Kenyan capital set to stage the event in Africa for the first time.
The World Athletics Council selected the two cities following its 242nd meeting on Monday and Tuesday. Nairobi was chosen for 2029 and Munich for 2031, while London and Rome missed out in the bidding process.
The 2031 championships will mark the third time Germany has hosted the event.
"We were fortunate to have four outstanding bids for 2029 and 2031, and this was not an easy decision for the Council," World Athletics President Sebastian Coe said.
"With this Council decision today, we continue to fulfil our mission of growing athletics worldwide with the hosting of our World Athletics Championships in Asia, Africa and Europe over its next three editions."
Beijing will host the championships in 2027, meaning the next three editions will take place on three different continents.
The 2027 championships will also mark the end of Coe's 12-year tenure as World Athletics president. Coe was first elected president in Beijing in 2015.
"Nairobi and Munich ultimately presented two compelling propositions," Coe said. "They offer very different but equally exciting opportunities for our sport, and I am delighted that we can now begin working with both cities to stage two world-class championships." ■

SHANGHAI, Sept. 15 (Xinhua) -- China's table tennis team will head to Japan on Wednesday targeting all seven gold medals at the Aichi-Nagoya Asian Games, while also looking to accelerate a generational transition ahead of the 2028 Los Angeles Olympics (LA28).
China won six table tennis gold medals at the 2023 Asian Games. Alongside Olympic champions Sun Yingsha, Wang Manyu and Wang Chuqin, this year's squad includes seven Asian Games debutants.
"The three Olympic champions pass on their experience to the seven young players, while the younger ones bring passion and drive to inspire the Olympic medalists," team head coach Qin Zhijian said.

Lin Shidong, preparing for his Asian Games debut, said training had helped him adapt to the competition venue and match balls as his physical condition continued to improve.
"I'm eager to hit top form at the Asian Games," Lin said.
Host Japan is expected to pose one of China's biggest challenges. Sora Matsushima, Tomokazu Harimoto and Miwa Harimoto are among a generation of Japanese players who have emerged as major contenders on both the men's and women's sides.
China men's head coach Wang Hao described the Asian Games as a midterm test ahead of the Los Angeles Olympics and an important opportunity for established players and younger athletes.
The rivalry has been particularly close in the women's team event. China came from 2-1 down to beat Japan in the women's team final at each of the past two World Table Tennis Championships.
China women's head coach Ma Lin said Japan had completed its own generational transition in recent years.
"We have never underestimated Japan. We have analyzed Japanese players' playing styles and know exactly what we are up against," Ma said. "We need to stay vigilant, treat every ball seriously and hold ourselves to strict standards in training."
Sun also expects another tight contest.
"We are closely matched. It all goes down to on-court performance and team cohesion. That counts most," she said.
Men's captain Wang Chuqin said China must maintain its confidence when facing difficult situations.
"We must hold firm to our belief in victory," Wang said. "We actively find ways to resolve difficulties, feed ourselves positive signals during play and free our minds mentally."
Ma said China's women would target every title available.
"We will go all out to fight for every gold medal," he said. "Huge challenges lie ahead, yet we have full confidence. Whatever obstacles come our way, we will fight to win."
Sun, competing at her third Asian Games, said she had set no specific individual target.
"Whenever I step onto the court, I will give my all to win," she said.
Wang Chuqin echoed that approach, saying he would give his best in every event assigned to him.
Qin said the Asian Games represented both an immediate test and another step toward shaping China's squad for Los Angeles, with the longer-term development of the team extending toward the 2032 Brisbane Olympics.
"We must maintain a positive mindset, stand united, focus on our own work and keep growing stronger," he said. ■

Forest fires burn over 48,000 hectares in Indonesia's West Kalimantan
China
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
20:37:28 CST
21:37:28 KST

JAKARTA, Sept. 15 (Xinhua) -- More than 48,000 hectares of land have been scorched by forest and land fires in Indonesia's West Kalimantan province since the beginning of the year, the National Disaster Management Agency (BNPB) said Tuesday.
According to Berton S. P. Panjaitan, acting head of the agency's Data, Information and Disaster Communication Center, satellite observations and field surveys put the total affected area at 48,535 hectares.
Nearly 14,760 personnel have been mobilized to contain the fires, supported by five patrol helicopters and nine aircraft conducting water-bombing operations, Berton said.
Authorities detected 19 fire hotspots on Monday, covering an estimated 261 hectares. Fires on about 180 hectares had been put out, while more than 80 hectares were still burning.
Smoke from the fires has pushed air quality to hazardous levels in Mempawah, Pontianak, Melawi, Sambas and Ketapang. Visibility in some locations has dropped to less than one kilometer, although several districts have seen rainfall in recent days, Berton said.
Forest and land fires occur regularly during the dry season in Indonesia's Sumatra and Kalimantan regions, with haze from the blazes sometimes reaching neighboring Southeast Asian countries. ■

Canada is seeking private investment through long-term concessions to operate the nation's four largest airports as part of a campaign to boost the economy, Prime Minister Mark Carney said on Tuesday.

Speaking on the sidelines of the summit, two asset managers told Reuters they would be interested in investing in airports.
Canadian labor groups have said they oppose privatization, arguing that it would raise costs for travelers.

====================================================================================================

Lebanon seeks UN-backed replacement for UNIFIL as US opposes mandate extension
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
14:00:48 UTC
23:00:48 KST

The Lebanese government is pressing members of the UN Security Council to establish a multinational force that would replace the UN Interim Force in Lebanon (UNIFIL), which currently serves as a buffer between Israeli and Lebanese forces and whose mandate is set to expire at the end of the year, two Western diplomats told The Jerusalem Post.
UNIFIL’s mandate in southern Lebanon is expected to expire at the end of the year under a UN Security Council resolution, after years of operating to monitor the implementation of Resolution 1701, which ended the Second Lebanon War. With the mandate’s expiration, responsibility for security and maintaining stability in southern Lebanon is expected to shift largely to the Lebanese Armed Forces (LAF).
UNIFIL is a UN peacekeeping force in southern Lebanon that was established in 1978 and operates under Security Council resolutions. Under its UN mandate, its primary role is to assist the Lebanese Armed Forces in ensuring that the area south of the Litani River remains free of weapons and armed personnel, in accordance with Resolution 1701.
The force consists of thousands of troops from various countries and conducts patrols, observation missions and operational activities alongside the Lebanese Armed Forces.
France pushes for extension
France is seeking to pressure the Security Council to extend UNIFIL’s mandate in southern Lebanon, a French official told the Post, saying the goal is “to avoid a vacuum and ensure a smooth transition to whatever comes next.”
In an effort to ease American opposition, France is arguing that the extension would be temporary. At this stage, however, Washington remains opposed to extending UNIFIL’s mandate.
The Lebanese government’s understanding that the US administration opposes extending UNIFIL’s mandate has led Beirut to propose a compromise: “Not a UNIFIL force, but rather a UNIFIL-like force that would serve as a buffer between Israel and Lebanon and remain in southern Lebanon.”
The Lebanese proposal calls for a force significantly smaller than the current UNIFIL deployment. Lebanese officials told senior US officials that “something needs to remain – some kind of force – in the area under Israeli control.”
Although the US has not yet agreed to the proposal, discussions are currently underway over the option, including the potential mandate and size of such a force, according to the two Western diplomats.
Israel opposes UNIFIL presence
The sides will have to reach a decision by November. Israel opposes the continued presence of an armed multinational force south of the Litani River, but there is concern that international pressure could ultimately lead the US, which holds veto power on the Security Council, to refrain from blocking the initiative.
“We have learned the hard way that UNIFIL forces do not help – they cause harm,” Israel’s Ambassador to the UN Danny Danon told the Post.
“By their very presence, they enable Hezbollah terrorists to return to various positions under the cover of UNIFIL activity. As a result, when UN forces are operating around us, it makes it more difficult for us to ensure that Hezbollah does not return to those positions. That is why we believe this mandate should end and the Lebanese Armed Forces should be allowed to be on the border and work with us.”
Danon stressed that Israel wants to work with the Lebanese Armed Forces in a manner similar to its coordination and cooperation with the Egyptian and Jordanian militaries.
“We believe they need to demonstrate independence not only in words, but also through actions,” he said.

====================================================================================================

Over 100,000 displaced by Houthis as escalation with Saudi-backed Yemeni government continues
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
14:00:40 UTC
23:00:40 KST

Over 100,000 people have been internally displaced as fighting continues to escalate along the West Coast between Iran-backed Houthi militia and Yemen’s Saudi-backed, internationally recognized government, the Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees announced on Tuesday.
“Humanitarian access remains a major challenge. Insecurity, damaged roads, telecommunications disruptions, and movement restrictions are affecting operations,” UNHCR said in a statement.
The latest escalation comes as the Houthis seek to seize control of the strategic Bab al-Mandeb Strait, a vital chokepoint for global oil and commodities shipping, and after the Houthis launched a number of attacks on Saudi Arabia and its assets. The attempt follows Iran’s efforts to assert control over the vital Strait of Hormuz.
The International Organization for Migration (IOM) already warned on Sunday that over 85,000 people had been displaced in Yemen, including 82,164 since the beginning of September alone, according to the Displacement Tracking Matrix (DTM).
More than 2,400 people fleeing Yemen’s escalating civil war have reached the northern shores of Djibouti so far, according to the UNHCR, as the civil war, which erupted after the Houthis seized Sanaa in 2014, continues to intensify. More than 60% of those fleeing are women, children, or elderly men.
Djibouti struggling with influx of displaced individuals
IOM and other UN partners have provided support to Djiboutian authorities, who have mobilized the Navy, army, police, and health services for the displaced masses. However, despite the support, Djibouti’s finance minister, Ilyas Dawaleh, said on Sunday that the situation was “becoming increasingly difficult for our country, despite our unwavering commitment to humanitarian resources.”
UNHCR Representative in Djibouti Sandrine Desamours said, “People are arriving after a difficult sea journey, many exhausted, distressed, and carrying what little they could take with them.”
She added: “The response is already under pressure. We urgently need more support before more families arrive and needs outpace our capacity to assist them.”
The IOM confirmed that new arrivals were being transported to the Markazi site in Obock, where they receive food and water, though Desamours warned that the site only has room for around 5,000 more refugees. The UN official said they were expecting in excess of 10,000 arrivals in the coming days and weeks.
IOM director-general calls for donor support for refugees
“Behind every number is a family who’s lost everything,” IOM Director General Amy Pope said. “Many have been displaced multiple times. People are crossing the sea with nothing because they have no other choice left. Yemen is a country torn by poverty and conflict, and it cannot carry this crisis alone. I call on donors to scale up support now, for those arriving in Djibouti and for the communities in Obock receiving them.”
Taiz, a province near the port city of Mocha on the Red Sea, remains the hardest hit, with 9,280 households, or nearly 56,000 people, displaced from its districts. Lahj follows, with 19,000 of its residents forced to flee, the majority from Al-Mudharibah district.

Probe Into Serbian Ruling Party’s Election Call Center Yields No Progress After Three Years
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
13:34:00 UTC
22:34:00 KST

Reported by
As Serbian President Aleksandar Vucic’s party gears up for elections at the end of October, suspicions of vote-buying in previous election cycles remain unresolved.
Serbian police have failed to question a single person in nearly three years of investigating an alleged vote-buying and illicit campaign finance scheme operated by the ruling Serbian Progressive Party (SNS), opting instead to transfer the case between internal departments, the Center for Investigative Journalism of Serbia (CINS) reported.
The stalled investigation takes on renewed significance as Serbia gears up for snap parliamentary elections on October 25. The political stakes were elevated further Monday evening when President Aleksandar Vučić announced on Pink TV that he will officially resign on September 27. Vučić stated he will address the nation to thank them for their support before entering the campaign as an “ordinary citizen” representing the SNS.
“The only thing I can say is that I faithfully served the citizens of this country, faithfully, diligently, devotedly, and with determination,” Vučić said.
The First Basic Public Prosecutor’s Office in Belgrade confirmed to CINS that, as of late August, the Ministry of Interior (MUP) had not interviewed anyone regarding the SNS call center case.
Prosecutors initially instructed police to investigate in December 2023 and sent three subsequent reminders, which went unanswered. Instead, on July 8, the MUP transferred the case from its economic crime department to its anti-corruption department. The MUP told CINS it will submit a report to prosecutors once its checks are complete.
The investigation stems from a November 2023 CINS undercover exposé of an SNS call center. A reporter documented that employment was conditional on voting for the ruling party and photographing the ballot as proof. Workers were allegedly organized by the hostess agency M&J Lady and received undeclared cash payments at the offices of a youth non-governmental organization, the Center for Education and Youth Development of Belgrade (CEROB).
The SNS, M&J Lady, and CEROB have all officially denied involvement to Serbia’s Anti-Corruption Agency. Despite the denials, the CINS investigation was cited in the final election report by the OSCE Office for Democratic Institutions and Human Rights (ODIHR) as an example of voter pressure and illicit third-party campaign financing.
Following the exposé, former opposition Members of Parliament Jelena Jerinić and Đorđe Pavićević filed criminal complaints alleging voting bribery, tax offenses, and unauthorized personal data collection. The case was briefly elevated to the Higher Public Prosecutor’s Office in Belgrade but was returned in early 2024 due to alleged insufficient evidence.
Experts have sharply criticized the prolonged delays. Nemanja Nenadić of Transparency Serbia called it one of the country's best-documented campaign finance violations, criticizing prosecutors for failing to direct the investigation or take action when police stalled. Retired police inspector Siniša Janković warned the police's inaction could constitute a criminal offense of abuse of office.
Vladimir Tupanjac, a lawyer and former deputy prosecutor, noted that prosecutors legally did not have to wait for the police and could have gathered the evidence themselves.
"Based on the conduct so far, one could conclude that the police and the prosecution are accomplices in covering up a crime," Tupanjac told CINS.

Kaunas to begin designing first tram line
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:37:00 UTC
21:37:00 KST

Lithuania’s second-largest city will begin designing its first tram line after Kaunas City Council approved an update to the city’s Sustainable Urban Mobility Plan on Tuesday, a municipal official said.
Martynas Matusevičius, head of the city’s Transport and Traffic Management Division, told BNS that the approval moves the tram project beyond the conceptual stage and formally incorporates it into the city’s strategic planning documents.
“Now we have the tram not only at the level of an idea, but also in a strategic document, the Sustainable Urban Mobility Plan,” Matusevičius said. “The next steps will be to prepare design proposals.”
The city expects to announce a tender for designing the tram line in the near future, he said.
The first project is likely to be a shorter route running along Šiaurės Avenue and Savanorių Avenue toward Kaunas Castle, Matusevičius said.
“There are other lines in the Sustainable Urban Mobility Plan as well,” he said. “We believe it would be best to start from this location.”
A feasibility study commissioned by the municipality previously examined four potential routes for a high-capacity public transportation system, with different options connecting the Eiguliai district with central Kaunas.
The city said last year that building a tram line could cost about 240 million euros. Kaunas Mayor Visvaldas Matijošaitis said at the time that the project would proceed if most of the construction costs could be covered by central government and European Union funding.
Matusevičius said the first line could also establish infrastructure that would benefit future expansions.
“This would be a major start because once the depot is built, another line would not need a separate depot,” he said.
The final cost of the project will become clearer during the design phase, Matusevičius said.
Kaunas, Lithuania’s second-largest city with a population of more than 300,000, currently relies primarily on buses and trolleybuses for public transportation.

EIB helps transform Lithuania’s port of Klaipėda into defence and clean-energy hub with €100 million in financing  - The Baltic Times
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:36:21 UTC
21:36:21 KST

- EIB to bolster defence and energy role of Lithuanian port of Klaipėda with €100 million loan
- Project to adapt cruise ship terminal to accommodate NATO vessels, strengthening military mobility in Baltic region
- EIB credit covers half of project, which also includes shoreside electricity, green hydrogen and offshore wind infrastructure
The European Investment Bank (EIB) is providing a €100 million loan to help transform Lithuania’s largest seaport, Klaipėda, into a strategic gateway for defence, energy and trade in the Baltic region. The Klaipėda State Seaport Authority will use the EIB loan to upgrade and expand infrastructure, strengthening the port's role in military mobility, increasing clean-energy facilities and supporting offshore wind development.
The location of the port, which dates to the 13th century, is important for North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO) operations. Parts of the civilian cruise-ship terminal are being adapted to meet NATO requirements, enabling more efficient logistical support for allied naval vessels. This dual-use approach strengthens military mobility while preserving the infrastructure’s value for civilian transport and the local economy. The whole project is scheduled for completion by the end of 2027.
The EIB credit will cover up to half the project's total cost of around €201 million, with the remaining financing to come from the European Union, national sources and other institutions. The project also includes shoreside electricity supply for ferries, green-hydrogen production and refuelling infrastructure, low-emission port vessels, upgraded quays for offshore wind development, dredging works and reinforced breakwaters.
"Few projects illustrate Europe's priorities as clearly as the Port of Klaipėda," said EIB Vice-President Karl Nehammer. "It is an investment that directly contributes to the missions of supporting European security and cohesion, while aligning with the green transition.”
The port is part of the EU's core Trans-European Transport Network (TEN-T). The upgraded quays and maritime infrastructure will help position Klaipėda as a key logistics hub for the deployment and maintenance of offshore wind farms, supporting Europe's green transition and energy security.
„Klaipėda Port is an important part of Lithuania’s infrastructure, supporting the country’s economy, energy security and national security,“ said Algis Latakas, Director General of the Klaipėda State Seaport Authority. “The financing agreement signed today with the EIB is an important step forward for the port’s development. It will help us deliver key projects that are shaping the future of Klaipėda Port – from the new dual-use Cruise Ship Terminal and shore power facilities to other investments that will strengthen the port’s competitiveness, sustainability and resilience.”
Background information
EIB Group
The European Investment Bank (EIB) Group is the financing arm of the European Union, owned by the 27 Member States, and one of the largest multilateral development banks in the world. In 2025, the EIB Group signed €100 billion in new financing and advisory services for over 870 high-impact projects under eight core priorities that support EU policy objectives: climate action and the environment, digitalisation and technological innovation, security and defence, territorial cohesion, agriculture and the bioeconomy, social infrastructure, strong global partnerships and the savings and investments union. Beyond long-term loans for large infrastructure, the EIB Group crowds in private investment for high-risk innovative projects and businesses, with a growing role in Europe’s markets for venture debt, venture capital, guarantees and securitisations.
Port of Klaipėda
The Port of Klaipėda is the most important national transport hub. It connects the sea, land and railway routes, it serves as the gateway of the State of Lithuanian to the world.
Klaipėda port is also a particularly important part of the Lithuanian economy, generating annual returns for every Lithuanian citizen and creating competitive conditions for business development.
Continuous investment of the Port of Klaipėda Authority in development and modernisation of the port has brought it into the ranks of the most advanced and competitive ports.
The Port of Klaipėda is directly connected with 70 countries worldwide. Klaipėda receives 6,000 ships each year, bringing in and out around 40 million tonnes of cargoes, and up to half a million tourists and passengers arrive to Lithuania by cruise and passenger ships. (816)

2026-09-15

21:35:59 KST

Luminor Bank has granted EUR 1.06 million in financing for the development of forestry, timber wholesale and manufacturing company RĒZEKNES MEŽS. The financing consists of a credit line to supplement the company’s working capital and a long-term loan. The financing will help the company increase timber purchasing volumes, develop production and expand its export markets.
The financing will be used for forest stand purchases, the purchase of timber and other raw materials, as well as financing the company’s day-to-day operations. The additional working capital will enable the company to maintain larger stocks of raw materials, take advantage of market opportunities in a timely manner and ensure an uninterrupted production process while awaiting payments from business partners. It will also enable the company to increase the production of higher value-added wood products and further develop sales in both domestic and export markets.
“RĒZEKNES MEŽS is one of the companies that has demonstrated rapid growth in recent years, significantly increasing its turnover and expanding its operations. For fast-growing manufacturing companies, it is important to be able to secure raw materials in a timely manner and maintain flexibility in order to take full advantage of market opportunities. The financing granted will provide the company with the resources needed for further growth and help it implement the next stage of its development,” says Ilze Zoltnere, Head of the Corporate Department at Luminor Bank in Latvia.
“In our industry, it is essential to be able to purchase forest stands and raw materials at the right time in order to ensure stable production and fulfill customer orders on time. The financing granted will enable us to increase purchasing volumes, build up larger stocks and respond more flexibly to demand in different markets, while continuing to grow our business,” says Elmārs Grišuļonoks, Member of the Management Board of RĒZEKNES MEŽS.
RĒZEKNES MEŽS has been operating in the forestry sector for more than 10 years. The company offers a full range of services – from forest purchasing and logging to timber transportation, roundwood processing and the production of lumber, wood chips and firewood. In recent years, the company has significantly expanded its operations, developing higher value-added products and strengthening its position both in the domestic market and in the European Union and Asia. In 2025, the company’s turnover reached EUR 5.27 million, which is 93 % more than a year earlier.
The company’s administration is based in Ludza, while its operations also extend to Cesvaine and Sigulda. As the company has expanded, it has increased its workforce by 15 % in recent years, employing 35 people at the end of 2025. Alongside developing its operations in Latvia, the company actively participates in international industry exhibitions and continues to expand cooperation with partners in the European Union and Asia.
About RĒZEKNES MEŽS
RĒZEKNES MEŽS is a wood processing company with many years of experience in the forestry sector. The company offers a full range of services – from forest purchasing and logging to roundwood processing, lumber production and export. The company’s production facility is located in Ludza and specialises in birch roundwood processing and the production of birch lumber for domestic and export markets.
About Luminor
Luminor is the leading independent bank in the Baltic states and the third largest provider of financial services in the region. We provide financial services to individuals, families and enterprises. We are new, dynamic and forward-looking just like Estonian, Latvian and Lithuanian markets.

====================================================================================================

Anthropic co-founder says mandatory AI ‘kill switch’ may be needed: Report
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:35:06 UTC
21:35:06 KST

Dilara Hamit
15 September 2026•Update: 15 September 2026
Companies developing advanced artificial intelligence may eventually need to be legally required to maintain an independently verifiable “kill switch” capable of shutting down dangerous systems, Anthropic co-founder Jack Clark has said.
Clark, one of the seven founders of the company behind the Claude chatbot, told the BBC that most major AI laboratories, including Anthropic, already had different ways to “pull the plug” on their systems.
He said, however, that governments may need to make such safeguards compulsory and require independent verification that they work as intended.
“Should you mandate for companies to definitely have a kill switch? Is that kill switch verifiable by a third party?” Clark asked.
“I think that’s the kind of thing society is going to want to know and might want to eventually pass rules around,” he added.
A kill switch would allow a developer or authorized outside body to completely disable an AI system if it began behaving dangerously or could no longer be reliably controlled.
Clark said the technical requirements, the people who would be authorized to activate such a mechanism and how it could be independently tested should form part of a broader policy discussion on AI.
His comments come amid renewed warnings from executives and researchers about the pace of AI development and the possibility that future systems could operate beyond human control.
Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei called over the weekend for AI development to slow down and undergo closer monitoring. He also proposed placing independent third-party watchdogs inside leading AI companies, while saying any response should avoid “sacrificing commercial advantage.”
Last week, a former Anthropic researcher resigned after accusing leading AI companies of failing to behave responsibly, with another researcher Evan Hubinger subsequently saying that he personally believed the risk of AI causing human extinction was greater than 10% within the next decade.

“We believe that space must be free of any weapon,” Kremlin spokesman Dmitry Peskov told reporters.
JOIN US ON TELEGRAM
Follow our coverage of the war on the @Kyivpost_official.
“We are counting on broad international consolidation to continue working toward the complete demilitarisation of space,” he added.
Russia’s ally China also on Tuesday slammed Washington’s move, and urged the United States to “stop preparing for war in outer space”.
The United States confirmed on Monday it had weapons deployed for the first time.

“Space control encapsulates the mission areas required to contest and control the space domain -- employing kinetic and non-kinetic means to affect adversary capabilities,” the statement said, adding that “these capabilities can be employed for offensive and defensive purposes.”
The 1967 Outer Space Treaty -- to which the United States, Russia and China are all parties -- bans the deployment of nuclear weapons in space, but is not a blanket ban on military activity.

Turkish Foreign Minister Hakan Fidan (L) and his Azerbaijani counterpart Ceyhun Bayramov (AA Photo)
Turkish Foreign Minister Hakan Fidan has once again urged Russia and Ukraine not to attack civilian vessels and disrupt maritime security in the Black Sea following a surge in incidents, reiterating Ankara’s continued dialogue with both warring sides to prevent the spread of the war.
Fidan made these remarks during a press conference in Baku alongside Azerbaijani Foreign Minister Ceyhun Bayramov on Sept. 15.
“The spread of the war into the Black Sea, threatening the lives of our citizens and the activities of our companies, is unacceptable. We are issuing the necessary warnings to the parties involved,” Fidan said.
The war between Russia and Ukraine has turned into a war of destruction as both sides do not hesitate to attack both strategic and civilian targets, Fidan said, expressing his concerns that this trend can be spread to the entire region, especially in the Black Sea.
“From the beginning of the war, our concerns and efforts were to prevent to spread the war to the Black Sea. However, the increasing attacks on civilian ships in the Black Sea in recent months are severely damaging the safety of life, property, navigation and the environment in the region. Unfortunately, our Turkish and Azerbaijani sailors have also lost their lives in these attacks,” he added.
Türkiye’s recent initiative for a moratorium in the Black Sea has not yet been materialized, Fidan said, but Ankara’s engagement with the parties will continue to this end.
“We believe that a ground for consensus can be achieved if the necessary political will is demonstrated and actions are taken in good faith. We have recently intensified our efforts to reach an agreement, and we are taking the necessary initiatives to bring it to a conclusion,” the minister suggested.
The international community and relevant actors should continue their efforts to this end, and try to help Russia and Ukraine through appropriate formulas and solutions, Fidan said, stressing Türkiye will lead all these efforts.
The disruption the maritime security has the capacity to further endanger food security and energy transfer to the world markets which can further increase oil prices in the world, Fidan said. “Of course, out of all these, the spread of the war is the last thing we want.”
Fidan and Bayramov also discussed other regional developments, including the war between Iran and the United States.
The Turkish top diplomat reiterated Ankara’s pressure on both sides to end the armed conflict and pave the way for free and uninterrupted use of the Strait of Hormuz in line with its pre-war status.
“The recently increasing mutual attacks are severely disrupting peace efforts. These actions, which escalate tensions in the region, must end immediately,” he said.

Researchers warn social media driving harmful misuse of Alzheimer’s blood tests
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:30:43 UTC
21:30:43 KST

Researchers have warned that social media may be misleading healthy people into taking Alzheimer’s blood tests, causing severe anxiety over biological markers that may never actually cause dementia.
Blood-based biomarker tests, such as p-tau217 and p-tau181, detect biological signs of changes in the brain associated with Alzheimer’s disease, which is a progressive brain disorder and the leading cause of dementia.
The testing looks for abnormal proteins, which can be a sign of brain changes due to Alzheimer’s. Much hope has been placed in the tests because they are often quicker and more accessible compared with other methods to diagnose Alzheimer’s, such as brain imaging or lumbar punctures.
There is global consensus among peak neurological bodies, including the Alzheimer’s Association, that such tests should never be used for healthy people with no symptoms.
The tests should not be used on their own to diagnose Alzheimer’s and need to be combined with other medical assessments, according to clinicians and peak bodies. Because they detect signs of Alzheimer’s early, a person can test positive for the biological markers of disease – but they may not ever go on to develop the clinical symptoms of dementia.
Dr Jenna Smith from the University of Sydney’s school of public health said this has not stopped laboratories, influencers and doctors around the world from promoting the tests on social media.
She and her colleagues analysed 188 posts mentioning Alzheimer’s or dementia and blood tests from accounts with a combined global audience of more than 114 million followers across Facebook, Instagram and TikTok.
Smith said about 10% of the posts were doctors from claiming the test could be useful for asymptomatic screening and that most of these were from the US.
“Some of these doctors had pretty clear financial interests,” Smith said. “But it isn’t all doom and gloom. Some doctors were sharing information about these new blood tests with longer videos and covered the nuance that this issue deserves. Some also specifically drew attention to the limitations and potential harms.”
The findings were published in the Journal of the American Geriatrics Society on Tuesday.
The researchers found more than one-third of posts promoted the tests for people without symptoms, and that accounts with explicit financial interests were more than three times as likely to do so.
“The main potential harms are being unnecessarily diagnosed and not having a clear pathway for what to do next,” Smith said.
“Healthy people who undergo these tests could be diagnosed with Alzheimer’s disease but not go on to develop dementia. It is also not clear what to do next after testing positive.
“Research on treatments has been done in people with mild cognitive impairment or dementia, so we don’t know if they work for healthy people who test positive.
“There are also possible side effects and costs of treatment to consider, and the psychological impact and burden of diagnosis on individuals, their families and communities, and insurance implications.”
The president of the Australian and New Zealand Society for Geriatric Medicine, Prof Susan Kurrle, said the tests should only ever be ordered by doctors in specific circumstances.
“From the evidence, it is clear that to ensure an accurate result there needs to be a high pre-test probability that the person has early Alzheimer’s disease before the test is performed,” she said.
“It is most definitely not a screening test. Any unnecessary and expensive tests are bad news.”
When ordered through a doctor in Australia, the tests cost up to $AU400 and are not covered by Medicare.
Patients seeking out the tests online are being charged $600, before undergoing an eligibility assessment. Another provider offers the test for $365 with no mention of pre-test screening.
Kurrle said there is a risk that a false positive result may lead people to believe that they are inevitably going to develop dementia.
“Alternatively a negative result may lead a person to think that they are not at risk and to not address the modifiable risk factors such as low levels of exercise, and the various vascular risk factors including LDL cholesterol, or hearing or vision loss,” she said.

Erdoğan vows to keep youth at center of political life
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:28:34 UTC
21:28:34 KST

Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan speaks with youth during the ‘Youth Meetings’ event in northern province of Samsun. (AA Photo)
President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan has pledged to put young people at the center of Turkish politics, saying their participation is a matter of political will and that they will carry responsibility for the country’s future.
“Putting young people at the center of politics, remember, is a matter of will,” Erdoğan said during a meeting with young people in the northern province of Samsun.
Erdoğan said his ruling Justice and Development Party (AKP) had worked with young people throughout its 25-year history and his more than five decades in politics.
He highlighted a constitutional amendment lowering the minimum age for parliamentary candidates from 30 to 18 as an example of efforts to expand young people’s role in politics. He also cited government support for youth in education, sports, public administration, culture, the arts and civil society.
“Tomorrow, you will take over the captain’s bridge of Türkiye,” Erdoğan told the gathering, saying young people “would be responsible for serving the country’s 86 million people as well as millions of people around the world who place their hopes in Türkiye.”
He urged young people to embrace a lasting sense of purpose rooted in history and cultural heritage, saying their generation should connect the present with the past.
Erdoğan also recalled the Sept. 12, 1980, military coup, whose 46th anniversary fell on the previous day, saying young people had been subjected to torture, imprisonment and executions during the period.
“Unfortunately, these coup-minded people consumed the young people of that period in this way. Many of those young people were killed,” Erdoğan said. “Our hope is that such things will not happen again.”
He called for an end to political violence and urged young people to build the country’s future through their work and contributions.
“Let no young people be killed, one from the right and one from the left,” he said. “Let everyone continue on their path with their heads held high, and let young people prepare the Türkiye of the future with their work and everything they have.”
He said the government’s aim was to prevent such abuses from recurring and pledged to continue meeting with young people to discuss their ideas, demands and needs.
“We will continue to embrace young people and walk alongside them,” he said.
Answering questions from young people after his speech, Erdoğan said his government’s “terror-free Türkiye” process had improved security in the country’s southeastern and eastern regions.
“Now the goal is to bring a peaceful atmosphere to the whole of the southeast and east on a larger scale,” he said.

Foreign Ministry Spokesperson Guo Jiakun’s Regular Press Conference on September 15, 2026
China
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
20:28:12 CST
21:28:12 KST

Special Advisor to the President of Brazil Celso Amorim will visit China from September 16 to 17. Member of the Political Bureau of the CPC Central Committee and Director of the Office of the Central Commission for Foreign Affairs Wang Yi will meet with him.
CCTV: The 2026 China International Fair for Trade in Services (CIFTIS) closed in Beijing two days ago. It attracted nearly 100 countries and international organizations to exhibit and hold events. A host of new products and services made their debut, drawing widespread attention. Some say CIFTIS has become a window into China’s continued opening up. What is your comment?
Guo Jiakun: “China Services” is a new calling card of China’s high-standard opening up. This year’s CIFTIS attracted over 1,830 offline exhibitors and over 5,300 online, reaching over 1,200 outcomes. A series of new technologies, new solutions and new achievements were exhibited in such areas as IT, financial technologies, digital health care, smart education and energy conservation and environmental protection, creating new opportunities for cooperation. China will continue to connect with the international market with premium services, promote outcome sharing through mutually beneficial cooperation, and bring “China Services” to more countries, delivering greater benefit to the world.
Kyodo News: The cross-party Japan-China Friendship Parliamentarians’ Union had a meeting with Assistant Foreign Minister Liu Bin yesterday. Can you share more details and how does China view the significance of this talk?
Guo Jiakun: On September 14, Assistant Foreign Minister Liu Bin met with the delegation of young Japanese lawmakers from the Japan-China Friendship Parliamentarians’ Union, who was on a visit to China. China stressed that the crux of the serious difficulties in China-Japan relations lies in Prime Minister of Japan Sanae Takaichi’s erroneous words and deeds on Taiwan. The Japanese side should honor the spirit of the four political documents, learn the lesson of history, and earn trust from its Asian neighbors and the rest of the world with concrete actions. We hope that the Japan-China Friendship Parliamentarians’ Union will carry forward its tradition of goodwill toward China, step up to its responsibility, and create conditions and a sound environment for bringing the bilateral relations back to the right track.
Global Times: It is reported that in a statement issued on September 11, the Philippines’ Department of Foreign Affairs reaffirms the Philippines’ sovereign rights and jurisdiction over Meiji Jiao and Zhubi Jiao as low-tide elevations (LTE) within the Philippines’ exclusive economic zone and continental shelf. Later, it changed the narrative, reaffirming its sovereignty over Zhubi Jiao as an LTE within the territorial sea of Zhongye Dao. The statement claims that China’s island-building on the above-mentioned features violates the Philippine Maritime Zones Act, UNCLOS and the 2016 Arbitral Award. Does China have any comment on that?
Guo Jiakun: Nansha Qundao, including Meiji Jiao, Zhubi Jiao and Zhongye Dao, are China’s inherent territory. It is totally legitimate and lawful for China to conduct construction and other activities on its own territory, a matter within China’s sovereignty.
The territorial scope of the Philippines has already been defined by a series of international treaties. Nansha Qundao has never been part of the Philippine territory. According to the “land dominates the sea” principle in international law, the Philippines’ claim of so-called maritime jurisdiction over China’s territory is illegal. The Philippine side at one point claimed that Zhubi Jiao is within its EEZ and continental shelf, but later claimed that it lies within the territorial sea of the island it illegally occupies. This only exposes how absurd and random its claim is, making it totally untenable.
The “South China Sea arbitration” is completely a political manipulation by the Philippines to grab unlawful gains. The so-called “award” is illegal, null and void, and has no binding force. The Philippines’ so-called “Maritime Zones Act” illegally includes China’s Huangyan Dao and most of the islands and reefs in Nansha Qundao and relevant waters into the Philippines’ maritime zone. This is a grave violation of China’s territorial sovereignty and maritime rights and interests in the South China Sea, and is illegal and null and void. China firmly opposes it and will never accept it.
Let me stress once again: China will continue to take resolute measures to safeguard its territorial sovereignty and maritime rights and interests, and firmly oppose any infringement. The Philippines should immediately stop infringing upon China’s rights, and stop the provocations and publicity stunts.
AFP: An official from the U.S. military said yesterday that the U.S. is facing a shortfall in ammunition as a result of its war in Iran. Does China have any comment on this?
Guo Jiakun: China is following the situation in the Middle East and Gulf region. We hope that relevant parties will resolve disputes through political and diplomatic means.
AFP: The U.S. Space Force said yesterday that the United States has weapons deployed in space. What is China’s reaction? Does China also have weapons deployed in space?
Guo Jiakun: China stands for peaceful use of outer space and keeping it safe, and opposes any arms race in outer space or any attempt to weaponize it and turn it into a warzone. We urge the U.S. to stop expanding military build-up in outer space, and uphold global strategic stability with concrete actions.
China News Service: According to reports, on September 14, the China National Space Administration, the Permanent Mission of China to the United Nations and Other International Organizations in Vienna, and the United Nations Office for Outer Space Affairs held a ceremony at the UN Headquarters in Vienna where China formally gifted the United Nations with the world’s first soil sample collected from the far side of the moon by the Chang’e‑6 mission. Ms. Monica Juma, Director‑General of the United Nations Office at Vienna, said that the permanent display of the lunar sample at the United Nations Office in Vienna is of great significance, adding that it demonstrates China’s support for the United Nations in galvanizing efforts in the international community to address common challenges. Would you like to comment on this? Is China trying to share its vision for space exploration through this gift?
Guo Jiakun: By donating to the United Nations a soil sample collected from the far side of the moon by the Chang’e‑6 mission, China is delivering on the four major global initiatives put forward by President Xi Jinping. The move demonstrates China’s firm support for the United Nations and multilateralism as well as its sense of responsibility as a major country on the peaceful use of outer space and improving global space governance. China looks forward to working with all countries to uphold relevant international law with the Outer Space Treaty as the cornerstone, abide by the paramount principle of exploring and using outer space for peaceful purposes, firmly oppose attempts to weaponize space or turn it into a battlefield, make sure progress in science and technology will bring greater benefits to all humanity, and ensure that space will truly become a platform for win‑win cooperation.
**************************************************
The following question was raised by Bloomberg after the press conference: Can the foreign ministry clarify and elaborate on your response yesterday regarding calls from U.S. tech leaders for a slowing down AI development. How does China assess the risks posed by AI including the possibility of AI being used for the production of biological weapons?
Guo Jiakun: We noted recent remarks by some in the U.S. AI industry. As AI develops at an incredible speed, it’s all the more important to stay committed to developing AI for the positive, for good, and for the benefits of the people, to calibrate regulation and governance on AI, and to promptly strengthen safeguards to prevent it from getting out of control.
China puts equal emphasis on development and security in terms of AI. We take seriously the inherent and secondary risks of AI. We are committed to holding on to the bottom line of security, and we have been making continued efforts to improve laws and regulations, policies, application norms, and ethic rules to prevent the abuse and misuse of AI, and ensure that AI is safe, reliable and controllable. Yesterday, Chinese relevant authorities released AI Safety Governance Framework 3.0, in which the classification of AI safety risks was reviewed and updated, and recommendations on optimizing technological countermeasures and comprehensive governance measures were outlined.
AI is the collective wisdom and precious asset shared by all humanity. AI development and governance require the practice of true multilateralism, with all countries working together to build a global governance framework on broad-based consensus. No single country or enterprise can develop effective solutions on its own. China always supports the UN’s role as the main channel in global AI governance, and participates in the multilateral governance of AI in a responsible attitude. China stands ready to join hands with all countries to build a just and equitable system for global AI governance, and develop AI for good and for all.

Heat pumps increasingly replacing oil-fired heating in Switzerland
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:28:00 UTC
21:28:00 KST

Heat pumps increasingly replacing oil-fired heating in Switzerland
A quarter of buildings in Switzerland were fitted with heat pumps by the end of 2025. Heating oil remains the main source of energy for heating, but its share is falling, the Federal Statistical Office (FSO) said on Tuesday.
As at December 31, the country had a total of 1.8 million residential buildings and 4.88 million dwellings, according to the FSO. More than one million of these buildings were detached houses, and 55% of them were occupied by just one or two people.
Half of these residential buildings were heated using fossil fuels. Heating oil, accounting for 33%, remains the main source of energy, although this figure has seen a “steady decline” over the last 40 years.
And 17% of buildings were heated by gas. This proportion varies greatly by region: gas heating accounted for 29% of dwellings in urban municipalities and only 4% in rural municipalities.
More
More heat pumps and less oil: how the Swiss heat their homes
Surge in heat pumps
The proportion of buildings connected to a heat pump (25%), meanwhile, has soared: it has increased sixfold since 2000 and now accounts for three-quarters of properties built over the last decade. Nearly a third of detached houses (30%) were fitted with a heat pump.
Furthermore, 12% of buildings were heated with wood and 7% with electricity.
The situation is slightly different when looking at households: in 2025, 58% of households in Switzerland were heated using fossil fuels (32% with heating oil and 25% with gas) and 22% with a heat pump.
More
Can heat pumps solve the climate crisis? Can they also cripple the Russian economy?
Average floor area: 102m²
The FSO also points out that the average living space per person was 46.6m². This figure had risen steadily until 2021 and has remained stable since then. The Lake Geneva region is below the national average, at 42.5m². Residents in Eastern Switzerland (49.7m²) and Ticino (49.8m²) have the largest living space per person.
The average floor area of an occupied dwelling is 102m². More than half (54%) of all dwellings have three or four rooms.
The average floor area of residential buildings is 174m². It has risen steadily for new-builds since 2000. Residential blocks built over the last ten years have an average floor area of 231m².

====================================================================================================

Polling turns politics into one big game. There are too many of them – and that does us no good | Jonathan Liew
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:27:56 UTC
21:27:56 KST

Solemn faces all round. We will listen. We will learn. We will hone and improve. Yes, it’s time for the latest bout of profound soul-searching in the UK polling industry following the allegations raised by the recent Channel 4 investigation that a polling firm reportedly failed to declare that three polls it published before the local elections in May were commissioned by Reform. By now these reckonings are becoming a regular occurrence in the wake of big elections: pollsters gather in a room, nibble biscuits, ritually self-flagellate, and pledge to learn all the right lessons from the electorate they’ve just catastrophically misread.
Yet for all its very public ignominies, political polling is one of those rare and precious industries that somehow manages to keep failing upwards. You might think that after botching the 2015 and 2017 general elections, the 2016 Brexit vote and the 2021 London mayoral poll, we might have learned better than to trust in the mystical magic graph. In this parallel universe Keir Starmer is now sitting on a colossal 318-seat majority, as forecast by Survation in its final MRP poll on the eve of the 2024 general election, overstating the actual result by a mere 144 parliamentary seats.
I am, of course, being slightly unfair. Most of the reputable pollsters enjoy just as many hits as misses – and are, by and large, staffed by smart and diligent people who genuinely care about their craft. Doubtless many among their number would object to any suggestion that the apparent misbehaviour of one firm – JL Partners, who were secretly filmed agreeing to carry out a poll favourable to Reform without disclosing who was commissioning it – can somehow be extrapolated to the wider profession as a whole. To which the only reasonable response is: well, let’s see how you like it.
And rest assured, I’ve read the Substacks. I’ve signed up to (the free version of) the newsletters. I’ve talked to polling experts. I’ve swished a curious finger up and down the interactive graphics. I’ve sat through interminable briefings in which a very nice man explains why he decided to carve up the voting public into 16 distinct blocs with catchy but absurd names: Flag Shaggers, Jealous Judies, Digital Wombats. And because Westminster is basically like the inside of a very small conch, these terms quickly pass into common parlance. “As you can see,” some wonk on the radio will opine, “the Tories are down eight points among the Angry Trevors, a whopping 13 with Disconsolate Flexitarians. But they are at least holding firm with Be At One Mums.”
But my problem has virtually nothing to do with methodology and almost everything to do with volume. There’s simply too much of this stuff, and people are taking it too seriously, investing too much predictive power. The British Polling Council (BPC) has swollen to 39 member organisations, many of them new and completely untested. In the first two weeks of September alone there have been 10 separate voting intention surveys issued by seven separate companies. We are all fatally beholden to the cult of Big Poll.
There are plenty of noble and legitimate defences of polls. They help to shape and test policy. They allow voters to make their choice with the fullest possible information. Perhaps they even function as a kind of check on an unfair electoral system, signposting to potential tactical switchers where their vote might profitably be spent. But even these defences highlight a paradox: this is a method of measuring and reflecting public opinion – and of subtly influencing it. Which is why so many lobbyists, PR firms and corporate interests are desperate to get involved.
Pollsters are not doing this out of some great altruistic duty to democracy. This is a business like any other, often using techniques borrowed wholesale from the market research and advertising sectors, and preying on a resource-starved, click-hungry media desperate for high-impact, low-cost content. “You aren’t going to pay for polling unless you are confident they can deliver the answer you want,” one public affairs executive told Politico last week. Or as George Gallup, one of the pioneers of modern-day polling, put it: “If it works for toothpaste, why not for politics?”
And so what started out as a small table in the newspaper every month has essentially become a biddable political actor in its own right, and there is a cosy symbiotic relationship between the people who conduct the polls, the people who pay for the polls and the people who publish the polls. This is how Reform can be treated like a government in waiting when they have eight MPs. This is how Starmer ends up as a lame duck within two years of his own landslide. In his 2018 memoir, the former FBI director James Comey admitted that his decision to reopen the investigation into Hillary Clinton’s emails may have been affected by the fact that she was well ahead of Donald Trump in the polls.
Beyond this, polls encourage us all to see politics as a kind of game within a game, a permanent rolling market ticker, in which the aim is not to improve the lives of human beings but to master the line on the graph, where winning elections is the end rather than the means. The BPC, with no fixed office and no full-time staff, was never going to fix this. In reality it is more of a trade body than a regulator, self-run and concerned less with ethics than trust and marketability.
And so JL Partners and the industry it belongs to are not the sole culprits here. In reality, the problem is the messenger: the people who consume and regurgitate politics for an audience, seduced by the idea that there is some all-seeing eye, a soup of numbers that will tell us all the answers. As someone whose very job deals in opinion, I know its fragility and malleability, the ridiculousness of treating it like a sacred stone tablet, or – I dunno – basing an entire generation of foreign and economic policy on how 635,000 people felt when they woke up on a Thursday in June 2016.
West Germany banned the death penalty in 1949 even though 77% of people were in favour of it. By 2000 that had fallen to 23%. Explanation, exemplar, persuasion, principle: these are the real tools of politics, at least a politics that seeks not to chase or triangulate public opinion but to mould and shape it. But if your entire existence is tied to a little line on a graph, then you’re never even going to try.
- 
  Jonathan Liew is a Guardian columnist

Iran: 'Woman, Life, Freedom' lives on despite war
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:27:00 UTC
21:27:00 KST

Iran: 'Woman, Life, Freedom' lives on despite war
September 15, 2026
A 22-year-old Kurdish woman, Jina Mahsa Amini, passed away in a Tehran hospital on September 16, 2022. She had left her home in northwestern Iran to visit the capital and was then confronted by Tehran's morality police, who claimed Amini was violating the hijab mandate ordering women to cover their hair. The police arrested her. Three days later, she was declared dead.
For many Iranians, the law enforcement's brutality towards women who allegedly broke the hijab rules is nothing new. And yet, Amini's death triggered an unprecedented outpouring of anger. Protesters all across the country took to the streets under the slogan "Woman, Life, Freedom." They marched for months, but the regime eventually managed to suppress the movement in a violent crackdown.
"From my perspective, the movement 'Woman, Life, Freedom' is the biggest social and cultural change in Iran in decades," said writer, poet and activist Pouran Nazemi.
Nazemi's work as a women's rights activist has gotten her arrested several times in recent years. Still, she refuses to be intimidated.
She told DW that the movement had clear goals right from the start: Ending the hijab mandate and ending state violence against Iran's population. While those goals were ultimately not met, the protests did trigger an important change — they prompted many people to recognize their own power within the society.
"The institutions still use physical violence and occasional arrests against us," said Nazemi. "But something fundamental has changed."
Hijab law still under debate
Even now, amid the ongoing war with the US, parts of the Iranian regime are still focusing on the hijab mandate. Many politicians are supporting a new bill which would introduce significantly stricter punishments for women violating the hijab order in public places.
One lawmaker described the missing head coverings as a "deep wound in the body of society."
The proposed law has been in limbo for years and marked by wrangling between various state institutions.
However, in a virtual parliamentary session on September 6, several lawmakers urged Parliamentary Speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf to pass the proposed legislation to the president for signing.
New normal for young Iranians
A 17-year-old female student told DW that she doesn't even think about wearing a hijab in public. Like many other young women, the pupil is posting pictures and videos of herself in colorful skirts and summer clothing on social media. To her, this is the new normal.
"My mother is not wearing a hijab either," the student told DW. "She is still a bit afraid and always carries a scarf so she can cover up in an emergency."
But even this degree of freedom would be hard to imagine just a few years ago. It was only after the 2022 protests that many woman started openly defying the government regulations and deciding for themselves if they wanted to cover up.
Decades of female resistance
The resistance of Iranian women did not start with the "Woman, Life, Freedom" protests. In its decades-long history, another important phase played out some 20 years ago, says women's rights activist and writer Mansoureh Shojaee.
In 2006, together with other Iranian activists and feminists, Shojaee started the "One Million Signatures" campaign. The goal was to gather 1 million signatures to change discriminatory laws targeting women. This campaign became an important role model for feminist activism and societal mobilization in Iran.
Activists went out into the streets, to universities, neighborhoods and private homes. They talked to people about divorce, custody, inheritance, court testimony, and polygamy. They also set up workshops, study groups and other cultural and media events.
Nobel Peace Prize winners among activists facing repression
From the very beginning, the initiative was met with massive state repression. Activists were summoned by the police, arrested and dragged through courts. Nobel Peace Prize winner Shirin Ebadi was among those affected, as was human rights activist Narges Mohammadi, who won the 2023 Nobel Peace Prize. Other activists, including Mansoureh Shojaee, were eventually forced to flee the country.
Shojaee left Iran in 2011 and is now living in the Netherlands.
The activists never met their original goal of reaching 1 million signatures. Still, they managed to change the conversation around the legal and social position of women. For this reason, Shojaee does not see the "Woman, Life, Freedom" movement as a full-on break with the past, but only as one of the chapters in the much longer story of Iran's resistance.
This article was originally published in German.

Cairo says Egyptian, Saudi national security ‘interconnected, inseparable’ after Houthi attacks
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-15
12:25:51 UTC
21:25:51 KST

Amer Fouad Solyman and Lina Altawell
15 September 2026•Update: 15 September 2026
Egypt on Tuesday affirmed its “full solidarity and complete support” for Saudi Arabia against any threat, saying the national security of the two countries is “interconnected and inseparable.”
The Egyptian Foreign Ministry statement came after Houthi drone and missile strikes on Saudi territory injured at least 13 civilians.
“The attacks constitute a clear violation of the rules and provisions of international law and a blatant threat to regional stability and security,” the ministry said.
It renewed Egypt’s “categorical rejection” of the Houthi attacks, warning that their continuation would fuel tensions and increase escalation in the region.
“The national security of Egypt and Saudi Arabia is interconnected, integrated and inseparable,” it stressed.
Earlier Tuesday, Turki al-Maliki, spokesperson for the Saudi-led coalition supporting Yemen’s internationally recognized government, said Tuesday that 13 civilians were injured in Houthi attacks targeting Khamis Mushait, Abha and Taif in the country’s southwest.
In recent days, the Houthis extended their control of Yemen’s Red Sea coastline, including Mayyun Island in the Bab el-Mandeb Strait.
Yemen has been gripped by war since the Houthis seized the capital Sanaa and several provinces in September 2014, prompting a Saudi-led Arab coalition to intervene in March 2015 in support of the internationally recognized Yemeni government.
